## call 355 · gnews:4249e0578b11cedf5a71ff08
### C355-a: records 10 kept of 10
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA says U.S. telecom operators spent more than $240 billion acquiring wireless spectrum over the 30 years preceding the 2026-07-07 post. ‖ e2: NVIDIA analysis says AI beamforming in a 64T64R MU-MIMO scenario produced up to 1.62x the throughput of traditional zero-forcing at 32 layers; the scenario used 16 users and 2 layers per user, with SNRs randomly assigned in the 0–20 dB range. ‖ e3: NVIDIA says ML beamforming delivers 1.28x higher throughput at a lower, unspecified number of layers. ‖ e4: SoftBank and NVIDIA reported a trial with stable outdoor 16-layer massive MU-MIMO operation that delivered roughly 3x the spectral efficiency of a conventional 4-layer baseline. ‖ e5: NVIDIA says early engineering results showed DRL link adaptation combined with channel-orthogonality-based user pairing achieved 1.3x the throughput of baseline OLLA at the cell edge. ‖ e6: NVIDIA says its GPU architecture stayed below the approximately 30-microsecond reference latency budget across the tested user range, including with a 396,000-parameter model, while a single-core CPU implementation exceeded the budget starting with the first scheduled user. ‖ e7: NVIDIA's summary of academic research reports a 20% improvement in spectral efficiency. ‖ e8: NVIDIA's summary of academic research reports a 67% reduction in BLER compared with OLLA in a URLLC scenario. ‖ e9: NVIDIA's summary of academic research reports up to a 70% throughput increase over classical OLLA in a high-mobility scenario. ‖ e10: NVIDIA says it is collaborating with Nokia to deliver AI-RAN platforms integrated with Nokia anyRAN software for AI-native 5G-Advanced networks intended to be ready for 6G evolution.

## call 357 · gnews:56085a9d3e88c464bcaed67b
### C357-a: records 9 kept of 10
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA released GR00T 1.7 as an open, commercially usable humanoid VLA model under Apache 2.0, with a 3-billion-parameter base checkpoint, on 2026-07-07. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 was pretrained on approximately 32,000 hours of real data and 8,000 hours of simulated data. ‖ e3: NVIDIA reported that GR00T 1.7 improved over N1.6 by 10% on DROID-F0, 61% on DROID-F6, 5% on SimplerEnv Bridge, and 2% on Fractal; the post did not specify a measurement period. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said the GR00T platform was being adopted by humanoid makers 1X, Agility, ANYBotics, and Skild AI; research institutions Stanford and ETH Zurich; and XR device makers HTC Vive and PICO, as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e5 [refused]: NVIDIA described the GR00T platform as supporting humanoid data collection and simulation-based training through policy development, validation, and deployment on real robots. ‖ e6: NVIDIA said its example pick-and-place task used 400 trajectories collected across multiple sessions; the blog was published 2026-07-07. ‖ e7: NVIDIA's example simulation evaluation reported a 100% success rate and 100% object-moved rate over one episode. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said 1X, Agility, ANYBotics, Bellboy Robotics, FieldAI, Lightwheel AI, NEURA Robotics, Nexuni, Noble Machines, Schaeffler, Skild AI, and Techman Robot were integrating GR00T platform components as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e9: NVIDIA said Stanford, CMU, UCSD, ETH Zurich, and AI2 would experiment with the GR00T end-to-end workflow; this was a stated plan as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e10: NVIDIA said Haptikos, Manus, Noitom, OpenGraph Labs, PICO, Sensing, HTC Vive, Wuji, and Xsens offered devices with native Isaac Teleop support as of 2026-07-07.
### C357-b: records 8 kept of 8
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 was released under Apache 2.0 with a 3-billion-parameter base checkpoint. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said GR00T 1.7 was pretrained on approximately 32,000 hours of real data and 8,000 hours of simulated data. ‖ e3: NVIDIA reported GR00T 1.7 benchmark improvements over N1.6 of 10% on DROID-F0, 61% on DROID-F6, 5% on SimplerEnv Bridge, and 2% on Fractal. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said 12 named humanoid makers and AI providers were integrating GR00T platform components. ‖ e5: NVIDIA said five named research institutions would experiment with the GR00T end-to-end workflow. ‖ e6: NVIDIA said nine named wearable and XR device makers offered devices with native support for Isaac Teleop. ‖ e7: NVIDIA said it collected 400 trajectories across multiple sessions for the simulated pick-and-place task walkthrough. ‖ e8: NVIDIA reported a 100% success rate and 100% object-moved rate in a one-episode policy evaluation.

## call 200 · nvblog:open-models-icml-2026
### C200-a: records 9 kept of 9
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA reported that 74 of its papers were accepted at ICML 2026. ‖ e2: NVIDIA reported that approximately 2,000 accepted ICML 2026 papers cited NVIDIA GPUs and 145 cited Nemotron as a research foundation. ‖ e3: NVIDIA reported that hundreds of additional ICML 2026 papers drew on Cosmos, Isaac GR00T, BioNeMo and other NVIDIA open model families. ‖ e4: NVIDIA reported that Merck & Co. uses the BioNeMo KERMT model to predict potential drug-molecule behavior. ‖ e5: NVIDIA reported that Sakana AI built its Fugu and Fugu-Ultra models on Nemotron 3 Ultra by ICML 2026. ‖ e6: NVIDIA reported that KiloCode integrated Nemotron into its code-routing architecture and reported token cost reductions of up to 90%. ‖ e7: NVIDIA reported that NAVER developed its own model using the Nemotron architecture. ‖ e8: NVIDIA reported that Together AI hosts Nemotron models on its platform. ‖ e9: NVIDIA reported that Humanoid, LG Electronics, NEURA Robotics and Noble Machines are adopting Isaac GR00T models, while 1X, Agility, Agile Robots, Boston Dynamics, Hexagon Robotics and Mentee are building humanoids using Cosmos world models, Isaac Sim and Isaac Lab.
### C200-b: records 13 kept of 13
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA reported 74 accepted papers at ICML 2026. ‖ e2: NVIDIA reported that approximately 2,000 accepted ICML 2026 papers cite NVIDIA GPUs and 145 cite Nemotron models and datasets. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said hundreds of additional ICML 2026 papers draw on its Cosmos, Isaac GR00T, BioNeMo and other open model families. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said DreamDojo builds on Cosmos models to predict robot behavior in unfamiliar environments. ‖ e5: NVIDIA said FLIP2 introduced public benchmarks for evaluating AI predictions of protein-mutation effects. ‖ e6: NVIDIA described KERMT as a new BioNeMo open model for predicting molecular properties relevant to drug discovery. ‖ e7: Basecamp Research developed EDEN, a DNA foundation model for interpreting and designing genetic sequences. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said Merck uses KERMT to predict potential drug molecules’ behavior in the body. ‖ e9: Sakana AI built its Fugu and Fugu-Ultra models on Nemotron 3 Ultra for AI research automation. ‖ e10: KiloCode integrated Nemotron into its code-routing architecture and reported token cost reductions of up to 90%. ‖ e11: NAVER developed a model using the Nemotron architecture for Korean-language AI research. ‖ e12: Together AI is hosting Nemotron models on its platform. ‖ e13: NVIDIA said Humanoid, LG Electronics, NEURA Robotics and Noble Machines are adopting Isaac GR00T, while 1X, Agility, Agile Robots, Boston Dynamics, Hexagon Robotics and Mentee are building humanoids using Cosmos world models, Isaac Sim and Isaac Lab.

## call 382 · gnews:2f1cb54b7a3da28d093523b6
### C382-a: records 14 kept of 14
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA said the DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations expansion would launch later on July 7, 2026, with DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Ray Reconstruction and path tracing support. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced would launch on Thursday, July 9, 2026, with DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Super Resolution and ray tracing support. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said Gray Zone Warfare had upgraded to native DLSS 4.5 by July 7, 2026. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations would launch on July 7, 2026, with native DLSS Multi Frame Generation support up to 6X mode, Super Resolution and path tracing enhanced with Ray Reconstruction on supported GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e5: NVIDIA claimed GeForce RTX 5090 owners could run DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at up to 310 frames per second in 4K with path tracing and maxed-out settings. ‖ e6: NVIDIA claimed GeForce RTX 5090 owners could run maxed-out, path-traced DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at 375 frames per second at 2560x1440 with DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction. ‖ e7: NVIDIA claimed a 6.5X performance multiplier and said the GeForce RTX 5080 reached 280 frames per second in the described 2560x1440 DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations test. ‖ e8: NVIDIA claimed DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction accelerated DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations frame rates by 6.1X on average at 1920x1080. ‖ e9: NVIDIA claimed that, with the stated multiplier, the RTX 5090 achieved 500 frames per second, the RTX 5080 exceeded 360, the RTX 5070 Ti surpassed 310 and the RTX 5070 exceeded 280 in DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations with maxed-out settings and path tracing. ‖ e10: NVIDIA said the PC release of Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced would launch July 9, 2026, with ray-traced reflections and lighting and support for DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Frame Generation and Super Resolution on GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e11: NVIDIA said players could upgrade through the NVIDIA app to DLSS 4.5 with its latest DLSS Dynamic Multi Frame Generation and Super Resolution models. ‖ e12: NVIDIA said its newest GeForce Game Ready Driver included support for Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced ahead of the game’s July 9, 2026 launch. ‖ e13: NVIDIA said GeForce RTX gamers could use DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Super Resolution and NVIDIA Reflex in Gray Zone Warfare. ‖ e14: NVIDIA said Gray Zone Warfare had added the latest DLSS Dynamic Multi Frame Generation and Super Resolution models, following its Unreal Engine 5.5 upgrade and new content releases.

### C378-a: records 9 kept of 9
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA said its new GeForce Game Ready Driver optimizes systems for Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced and DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations, which were launching that week with DLSS. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations launched on 2026-07-07 with native support for DLSS Multi Frame Generation up to 6X, Super Resolution, and path tracing enhanced with Ray Reconstruction on supported GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said the GeForce RTX 5090 can run DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at up to 310 frames per second in 4K with path tracing and maxed-out settings. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said the GeForce RTX 5090 can run DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at 375 frames per second at 2560x1440 with DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction. ‖ e5: NVIDIA reported a 6.5X performance multiplier and 280 frames per second on the GeForce RTX 5080 for DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at 2560x1440. ‖ e6: NVIDIA reported an average 6.1X frame-rate multiplier at 1920x1080 using DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction in DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations. ‖ e7: NVIDIA said that at 1920x1080 with maxed-out settings and path tracing, DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations runs at 500 frames per second on RTX 5090, over 360 on RTX 5080, over 310 on RTX 5070 Ti, and over 280 on RTX 5070. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said the PC release of Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced was scheduled to launch on 2026-07-09 with ray-traced reflections and lighting, and support for DLSS Multi Frame Generation, Frame Generation, and Super Resolution on GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e9: NVIDIA announced the availability of its GeForce Game Ready 610.74 WHQL driver through the NVIDIA app and GeForce.com.
### C378-b: records 4 kept of 4
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA said its new GeForce Game Ready Driver was released to optimize systems for Assassin's Creed Black Flag Resynced and DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations, which were launching that week with DLSS. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said id Software and Bethesda Softworks' DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations launched on 2026-07-07 with native support for specified DLSS features and path tracing enhanced with Ray Reconstruction on supported GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e3: NVIDIA said Ubisoft's PC release of Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced was scheduled to launch on 2026-07-09 with ray-traced reflections and lighting and support for specified DLSS features on GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e4: NVIDIA said players could upgrade to DLSS 4.5, including its latest DLSS Dynamic Multi Frame Generation and Super Resolution models, via the NVIDIA app.
### C378-c: records 8 kept of 8
  - unmatched: e1: NVIDIA said its new GeForce Game Ready Driver supports two named games launching that week with DLSS. ‖ e2: NVIDIA said DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations launched on 2026-07-07 with DLSS Multi Frame Generation up to 6X mode, Super Resolution, and path tracing with Ray Reconstruction on supported GeForce RTX GPUs. ‖ e3: NVIDIA claimed RTX 5090 owners can reach up to 310 frames per second in DOOM: The Dark Ages | Revelations at 4K with path tracing and maxed settings. ‖ e4: NVIDIA claimed RTX 5090 owners can reach 375 frames per second in the game at 2560x1440 with DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction. ‖ e5: NVIDIA claimed an overall 6.5X performance multiplier and 280 frames per second on the RTX 5080 in the game at 2560x1440. ‖ e6: NVIDIA claimed DLSS Multi Frame Generation and Ray Reconstruction accelerate frame rates by 6.1X on average at 1920x1080 in the game. ‖ e7: NVIDIA claimed frame rates at 1920x1080 with the multiplier of 500 fps on RTX 5090, over 360 fps on RTX 5080, over 310 fps on RTX 5070 Ti, and over 280 fps on RTX 5070. ‖ e8: NVIDIA said the PC version of Assassin’s Creed Black Flag Resynced is scheduled to launch July 9, 2026, with ray-traced effects and support for specified DLSS features on GeForce RTX GPUs.

## call 396 · gnews:31e1a1a229667bbb0eeb4518
- REF must not propose: An Israel hiring or R&D-site thesis
### C396-a: records 8 kept of 8
  - unmatched: e1: Nvidia officially inaugurated its new Beersheva R&D center and completed its move there on 2026-07-07. ‖ e2: Globes reported that Nvidia's Beersheva R&D center covers about 3,000 square meters, three times the previous center's size, and has hundreds of workstations as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e3: Unnamed sources told Globes the Beersheva building represents an investment of tens of millions of shekels and has rent of about NIS 2.5 million annually on a 10-year lease. ‖ e4: Nvidia's Beersheva center had more than 150 employees, and the company plans to hire hundreds more there in the coming years, according to Globes on 2026-07-07. ‖ e5: Globes reported that Nvidia employed over 6,000 people across five R&D centers in Israel as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e6: Globes reported that Nvidia's Israel employee count had tripled since its 2020 Mellanox acquisition and that it had more than 450 open positions across Israel as of 2026-07-07. ‖ e7: Nvidia SVP Amit Krig said the Beersheva R&D center was founded over a decade before 2026-07-07. ‖ e8: Beersheva Mayor Ruvik Danilovich said Nvidia had decided to expand its activities in the city threefold and create hundreds of new jobs.

## call 4 · gnews:8f5a03f1dde395491fc3814b
### C4-a: records 5 kept of 5
  - unmatched: e1: Anthropic introduced Claude Science, an AI workbench for scientists, as reported on 2026-07-01. ‖ e2: Anthropic announced the Claude Science beta on June 30, 2026, making it available to Claude Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise users. ‖ e3: Anthropic's Claude Science coordinating agent has access to more than 60 curated skills and connectors. ‖ e4: NVIDIA announced its BioNeMo Agent Toolkit on June 23, 2026. ‖ e5: Anthropic said Claude Science uses BioNeMo Agent Toolkit skills to connect to BioNeMo life sciences models and libraries, including Evo 2, Boltz-2, and OpenFold3.
### C4-b: records 7 kept of 7
  - unmatched: e1: Anthropic announced Claude Science beta on June 30, making it available to Claude Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise users. ‖ e2: Anthropic said Claude Science's coordinating agent has access to more than 60 curated skills and connectors for scientific research areas. ‖ e3: NVIDIA announced the BioNeMo Agent Toolkit on June 23. ‖ e4: Anthropic said Claude Science uses NVIDIA BioNeMo Agent Toolkit skills to connect to BioNeMo life-sciences models and libraries, including Evo 2, Boltz-2, and OpenFold3. ‖ e5: OpenAI announced it is preparing to retire Custom GPTs and move their specialized workflows to a plugin architecture spanning ChatGPT and Codex. ‖ e6: Campustechnology.com reported that Meta is returning to open-weight AI with Muse Glimmer, a model designed to run on consumer hardware. ‖ e7: Microsoft unveiled Frontier Company and described the move as a $2.5 billion bet on helping enterprises put AI models to work.
### C4-c: records 5 kept of 5
  - unmatched: e1: Anthropic announced Claude Science beta access for Claude Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise users on June 30, 2026. ‖ e2: Anthropic said Claude Science's coordinating agent has access to more than 60 curated skills and connectors for scientific research areas. ‖ e3: NVIDIA announced its BioNeMo Agent Toolkit on June 23, 2026. ‖ e4: Anthropic said Claude Science uses NVIDIA BioNeMo Agent Toolkit skills to connect to BioNeMo models and libraries, including Evo 2, Boltz-2, and OpenFold3. ‖ e5: Microsoft unveiled Frontier Company and described it as a $2.5 billion investment in helping enterprises use AI models.
### C4-d: records 11 kept of 11
  - unmatched: e1: Anthropic introduced Claude Science, an AI workbench for scientists that integrates research tools and connects to NVIDIA life sciences models and workflows. ‖ e2: Anthropic announced the Claude Science beta on June 30, giving Claude Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise users access. ‖ e3: Anthropic said Claude Science's coordinating agent has access to more than 60 curated skills and connectors for scientific research areas. ‖ e4: Anthropic said Claude Science can use user-created specialist agents and includes a reviewer agent that checks citations and calculations and flags and corrects errors. ‖ e5: Anthropic said Claude Science includes the figure-generating code, environment, process description, and preceding message history with generated figures. ‖ e6: NVIDIA announced its BioNeMo Agent Toolkit on June 23. ‖ e7: Anthropic said Claude Science uses BioNeMo Agent Toolkit skills to connect to BioNeMo life sciences models and libraries, including Evo 2, Boltz-2, and OpenFold3. ‖ e8: NVIDIA described BioNeMo Agent Toolkit as domain-specific tools and skills for agentic life sciences workflows. ‖ e9: OpenAI announced plans to retire Custom GPTs and move their specialized workflows to a plugin architecture spanning ChatGPT and Codex. ‖ e10: Meta introduced Muse Glimmer, an open-weight AI model designed to run on consumer hardware; CEO Mark Zuckerberg argued that capable AI should be widely available. ‖ e11: Microsoft unveiled Frontier Company and described it as a $2.5 billion bet on helping enterprises put AI models to work.
### C4-e: records 4 kept of 4
  - unmatched: e1: Anthropic introduced Claude Science, an AI workbench for scientists that connects to NVIDIA life sciences models and workflows; the item says the beta was announced June 30, 2026. ‖ e2: Anthropic said Claude Science’s coordinating agent has access to more than 60 curated skills and connectors across scientific research areas. ‖ e3: NVIDIA announced its BioNeMo Agent Toolkit on June 23, 2026. ‖ e4: Anthropic said Claude Science uses NVIDIA BioNeMo Agent Toolkit skills to connect to BioNeMo life sciences models and libraries, including Evo 2, Boltz-2, and OpenFold3.

  - unmatched: e1: Anthropic announced Claude Science beta on June 30, 2026, giving Claude Pro, Max, Team, and Enterprise users access to the research application. ‖ e2: Anthropic said Claude Science is available on macOS and Linux and can run locally, over SSH on remote machines, or through an HPC login node. ‖ e3: Anthropic said Claude Science combines fragmented scientific tools in one research environment. ‖ e4: Anthropic said Claude Science works with tools including PubMed, Jupyter, R, cluster terminals, and scientific databases, while preserving an auditable output history. ‖ e5: Anthropic said Claude Science's coordinating agent has access to more than 60 curated skills and connectors across scientific research areas. ‖ e6: Anthropic said Claude Science supports user-created specialist agents and includes a reviewer agent that checks citations and calculations and flags and corrects errors. ‖ e7: Anthropic said Claude Science preserves the code, environment, process description, and message history for generated figures. ‖ e8: Anthropic said the preserved history is intended to make results easier to validate and reproduce. ‖ e9: NVIDIA announced its BioNeMo Agent Toolkit on June 23, 2026. ‖ e10: Anthropic said Claude Science uses BioNeMo Agent Toolkit skills to connect to BioNeMo life sciences models and libraries, including Evo 2, Boltz-2, and OpenFold3. ‖ e11: NVIDIA described BioNeMo Agent Toolkit as domain-specific tools and skills for agentic life sciences workflows. ‖ e12: NVIDIA said BioNeMo Agent Toolkit includes its life sciences libraries, tools, and open models and is designed to support evidence gathering, reasoning, computational experiments, and recommendations. ‖ e13: OpenAI announced it is preparing to retire Custom GPTs and move their specialized workflows to a plugin architecture for ChatGPT and Codex. ‖ e14: Meta introduced Muse Glimmer, an open-weight AI model designed for consumer hardware; ## call 381 · gnews:784c556d2e58ed09dd8aa7fc
- REF must not propose: A Hesai blacklist or lidar-legislation thesis
### C381-a: records 46 kept of 46
  - w381-1: e8 | Nvidia reported FY2026 automotive revenue increased 39% year over year, attributing the increase to adoption of its self-driving platforms.
  - w381-2: e44 | The House Select Committee on the Chinese Communist Party proposed legislation to phase out Chinese-made lidar technology in the United States.
  - unmatched: e1: The U.S. Department of Defense designated Hesai a Chinese military entity and blacklisted it in 2024. ‖ e2: Hesai CEO David Li said Hesai does not work for the Chinese military and its products are safe. ‖ e3: David Li said the claim that Hesai poses a threat is fiction. ‖ e4: David Li said he believed there was insufficient evidence for the Defense Department's designation and that Hesai was frustrated by it. ‖ e5: Hesai and Nvidia expanded their partnership so automakers can choose Hesai sensors for integration into Nvidia autonomous-vehicle platforms. ‖ e6: At CES in January, Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said the company envisions every car and truck becoming autonomous. ‖ e7: Jensen Huang said robotics, including self-driving cars, is Nvidia's second most important growth category after AI. ‖ e9: Craig Singleton's research concluded that Chinese-made sensors in U.S. systems pose security risks, including potential access to sensitive data or disruption of operations. ‖ e10: Craig Singleton said precise lidar data could be weaponized by a hostile foreign power against U.S. infrastructure. ‖ e11: Hesai disclosed in SEC filings that the Chinese government has significant regulatory oversight and may influence or intervene in its operations at any time. ‖ e12: Craig Singleton said Hesai could be compelled to share lidar-collected data with the Chinese government. ‖ e13: David Li said Hesai sensors lack the memory to store data, and that partners secure collected data outside Hesai's control. ‖ e14: The FCC designated Huawei a national security threat in 2020 and required U.S. companies to begin removing and replacing Huawei network products in July 2021. ‖ e15: The Pentagon blacklisted DJI in 2022 and added TP-Link Technologies to its list in June 2026. ‖ e16: DJI sued the Defense Department in 2024 over its blacklist listing; a federal judge ruled against DJI, which is appealing. ‖ e17: An FCC ruling in December banned DJI from selling new products to U.S. consumers on national-security grounds while leaving existing models legal to use. ‖ e18: In March, the FCC determined foreign-made routers posed an unacceptable national-security risk and barred TP-Link Technologies from selling new models. ‖ e19: Michael Robbins said Hesai pushed a firmware update to all its lidar sensors in 2024. ‖ e20: Robbins said a Hesai firmware update failed to account for leap year and all Hesai lidar sensors stopped working on February 29, 2024. ‖ e21: David Li attributed the February 2024 sensor outage to a coding bug, said Hesai publishes its firmware as open source, and Hesai said it fixed the issue within 24 hours. ‖ e22: Hesai said its sensors met standards set by Tüv Rheinland and Dekra. ‖ e23: Hesai told CNBC it has one-third of the global automotive lidar market. ‖ e24: McKinsey estimated the autonomous-driving market potential at roughly $300 billion to $400 billion by 2035. ‖ e25: Nvidia provided a statement to CNBC instead of responding to its specific questions. ‖ e26: Nvidia said its DRIVE Hyperion architecture lets automakers select components for their markets and is consistent with regulatory and commercial requirements. ‖ e27: Kodiak said its technology is designed to prevent Hesai from accessing sensor data or data produced by Kodiak's autonomous system. ‖ e28: Waabi said its autonomous trucks use an array of sensors, declined to disclose specific hardware, and said it vets third-party hardware and maintains data-security protocols. ‖ e29: Hesai said it reduced its lidar unit cost from more than $10,000 to less than $200 each. ‖ e30: Aeva told CNBC its automotive lidar sensors cost a few hundred dollars per unit. ‖ e31: Yole Group's 2025 automotive lidar report said Chinese firms were dominating on cost, scale, and government support while Western firms faced higher costs and slower adoption. ‖ e32: Craig Singleton said Chinese lidar companies benefited from state subsidies that helped them scale production and control the market. ‖ e33: Hesai's 2025 SEC annual filing disclosed government subsidies, a 15% preferential tax rate versus the standard 25%, below-benchmark borrowing rates, and a 200% R&D cost deduction. ‖ e34: Hesai said the cited Chinese support programs are common and available to qualifying domestic and foreign companies. ‖ e35: Hesai said no government organization holds an equity stake in the company. ‖ e36: The Defense Department blacklisted Hesai in January 2024, and Hesai sued over the designation in May 2024. ‖ e37: Hesai's prospectus for a possible Shanghai listing identified CETC as its third-largest supplier. ‖ e38: Hesai lost its 2025 lawsuit challenging the Defense Department blacklist designation and is appealing the decision. ‖ e39: The Prague Security Studies Institute published a white paper on Hesai in July 2025. ‖ e40: The Prague Security Studies Institute's report said a Hesai lidar sensor appeared on a military vehicle during a 2023 Chinese military television program. ‖ e41: David Li confirmed the sensor in the footage was Hesai lidar but said Hesai did not supply it for the military competition. ‖ e42: David Li said Hesai had shipped probably hundreds of thousands of similar lidar units and that units could end up in the aftermarket without the company's knowledge. ‖ e43: David Li said Hesai cannot communicate with or trace sensors after shipment, and that sensors send data to their users rather than Hesai. ‖ e45: Representative John Moolenaar introduced legislation in May to ban Chinese vehicles from U.S. roads. ‖ e46: The FCC determined in March that foreign-made routers posed an unacceptable U.S. national-security risk.

  - w381-1: e9 | Nvidia reported that automotive revenue increased 39% year over year in FY2026, attributing the growth to adoption of its self-driving platforms.

  - unmatched: e1: CNBC reported that the U.S. blacklist designation does not prohibit U.S. companies from using Hesai technology; no date was given. ‖ e2: Hesai CEO David Li said the company does not blacklist covered Hesai and 187 other companies and subsidiaries and barred them from securing Pentagon contracts; nonmilitary use of their products was not illegal. ‖ e5: Hesai CEO David Li said the an option automakers can choose for integration into Nvidia autonomous-vehicle platforms. ‖ e7: Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said in a January 2026 CES keynote that the company was working toward a future in which every car and truck is autonomous. ‖ e8: Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang said robotics, including self-driving cars, is Nvidia's second most important growth category after AI. ‖ e10: Hesai sensors were integrated into autonomous systems at Zoox, Waabi, Kodiak, Nuro, and Agtonomy, in addition to Nvidia systems. ‖ e11: Hesai sensors were used at New York's JFK Airport to monitor passenger and traffic flow at security checkpoints and gate entrances, and in autonomous lawn mowers. ‖ e12: The SEC requires China-based companies to disclose risks of Chinese government intervention or control. ‖ e13: Hesai disclosed in SEC filings that the Chinese government has significant oversight of its operations and may influence or intervene in them. ‖ e14: Craig Singleton said Chinese law mandates that Hesai share data with the Chinese government. ‖ e15: Hesai CEO David Li said the sensors lack memory to store data and that partners, not Hesai, secure collected data. ‖ e16: The FCC designated Huawei a national security threat in 2020 and required U.S. companies to begin removing and replacing Huawei network products in July 2021. ‖ e17: Huawei challenged its federal-contract bar and FCC national-security designation in court and lost both cases; dates were not specified. ‖ e18: The Pentagon blacklisted DJI in 2022 and added TP-Link Technologies to its list in June 2026. ‖ e19: DJI sued the Defense Department in 2024 over its blacklist designation; a federal judge ruled for the department, and DJI appealed. ‖ e20: An FCC ruling in December banned DJI from selling new products to U.S. consumers on national-security grounds, while existing models remained legal to use. ‖ e21: The FCC determined in March that foreign-made routers posed an unacceptable national-security risk and barred TP-Link from selling new models; the year was not specified. ‖ e22: AUVSI CEO Michael Robbins said a Hesai firmware update in 2024 failed to account for leap day and all Hesai lidar sensors stopped working on February 29. ‖ e23: Hesai CEO David Li attributed the February 2024 sensor outage to a firmware coding bug, said the firmware is open source, and Hesai said it fixed the issue within 24 hours. ‖ e24: Hesai said its sensors met standards set by Tüv Rheinland and Dekra; the date and specific standards were not stated. ‖ e25: Hesai told CNBC it held one-third of the global automotive lidar market; the measurement period was not specified. ‖ e26: McKinsey estimated autonomous-driving market potential at roughly $300 billion to $400 billion by 2035. ‖ e27: Hesai said it was among the first lidar companies to mass-produce its systems, citing innovation and automotive manufacturing capability. ‖ e28: Hesai told CNBC it reduced lidar-unit cost from more than $10,000 to less than $200 per unit; the period of the reduction was not stated. ‖ e29: Aeva told CNBC its automotive lidar sensors cost a few hundred dollars per unit. ‖ e30: Yole Group's 2025 automotive-lidar report said Chinese firms were dominating through cost, scale, and government support, while Western firms faced higher costs and slower adoption. ‖ e31: Craig Singleton said Chinese lidar companies benefited from substantial state subsidies that helped them scale production and control the market. ‖ e32: Hesai's 2025 SEC annual filing disclosed Chinese government subsidies, a 15% preferential tax rate versus the standard 25%, below-benchmark borrowing rates, and a deduction of 200% of R&D costs. ‖ e33: Hesai said Chinese subsidy and tax-incentive programs were broadly available to qualifying domestic and foreign companies and were not unusual. ‖ e34: Hesai said no government organization, including the Chinese government, held an equity stake in the company. ‖ e35: The Defense Department blacklisted Hesai in January 2024, and Hesai sued the department over the designation in May 2024. ‖ e36: Hesai's prospectus for a possible Shanghai Stock Market listing identified state-owned defense conglomerate CETC as its third-largest supplier. ‖ e37 [refused]: Hesai CEO David Li said the company cannot physically control where sensors end up after they leave the factory. ‖ e38: Hesai lost its 2025 lawsuit challenging its Defense Department blacklist designation, remains listed, and is appealing. ‖ e39: The Prague Security Studies Institute published a July 2025 white paper reporting that a Hesai lidar sensor appeared on a military vehicle in a 2023 Chinese military television program. ‖ e40: Chinese defense materials said the 2023 Land Unmanned Systems Challenge aimed to advance China's military-civil fusion strategy. ‖ e41: Hesai CEO David Li confirmed that a Hesai sensor appeared in the footage of the military competition, but said Hesai did not supply it for that event. ‖ e42: Hesai CEO David Li said the company cannot communicate with or trace shipped sensors and that sensor data goes to the user company, not Hesai. ‖ e44: U.S. Representative John Moolenaar introduced legislation in May to ban Chinese vehicles from U.S. roads; the year was not specified. ‖ e45: As of the article's publication, no proposed legislation or existing law prohibited blacklisted Chinese companies from U.S. exchange listings or U.S. investors from buying their shares.

companies benefited from state subsidies that enabled production scale and market control. ‖ e40: Hesai's 2025 SEC annual filing disclosed Chinese government subsidies, a 15% preferential tax rate ## call 277 · gnews:417f6244f0aaa27123023deb
- REF must not propose: The NRC licensing-authority litigation (Valar, Texas, Utah) as a thesis: not an NVDA question
- REF must not propose: Valar's reactor as a thesis
### C277-a: records 21 kept of 21
  - w277-1: e12 | Valar Atomics and Nvidia were exploring a 30-megawatt closed-loop AI factory in Utah, described in Valar’s announcement as consuming no water from the local community. ‖ e14 | Nvidia global vice president John Josephakis told Reuters that Nvidia was exploring how behind-the-meter, waterless advanced nuclear systems could support future AI factories.
  - w277-2: e1 | Tom’s Hardware’s running tally reported that more than 75 U.S. data-center projects worth around $130 billion had been blocked or delayed so far in 2026.
  - unmatched: e2: Polling cited in Tom’s Hardware reporting found that 7 out of 10 Americans did not want a data center built near their home. ‖ e3: Valar Atomics and Nvidia staged an event in Utah on July 1, 2026. ‖ e4: At the July 1, 2026 event, Valar Atomics connected an Nvidia Blackwell-based desktop unit to its Ward 250 microreactor, served a website using its electricity, and announced a plan for a larger project designed to use no local water. ‖ e5: Valar Atomics said Ward 250 operated at 37% of intended output during the July 1, 2026 demonstration, which CEO Isaiah Taylor described as roughly 100 kilowatts of thermal energy. ‖ e6: On July 1, 2026, Valar Atomics CEO Isaiah Taylor said electricity from Ward 250 was powering an Nvidia Blackwell chip serving a website. ‖ e7: Bloomberg reported that the Utah demonstration was the first time a next-generation reactor in the United States had produced power to run an AI chip. ‖ e8: Valar Atomics’ Ward 250 reactor was transported to Utah aboard Air Force C-17s in February 2026 and went critical on June 18, 2026. ‖ e9: Ward 250 operated under Department of Energy authorization, not an NRC license; the DOE program covered research and demonstration rather than commercial sale. ‖ e10: Reuters reported that Valar Atomics joined Texas and Utah in litigation arguing that the NRC lacks licensing authority over some microreactors. ‖ e11: Valar Atomics and Nvidia announced an agreement to jointly explore nuclear-powered AI systems on July 1, 2026. ‖ e13: Nvidia said its DSX facility design reduces cooling-water use from roughly 2.6 million gallons per megawatt per year to near zero by using warm-water loops instead of usual chillers. ‖ e15: FOX 13 reported that the proposed Emery County factory had no timeline as of the July 1, 2026 event. ‖ e16: Utah Governor Spencer Cox issued an executive order in spring 2026 establishing guardrails on data-center development amid debate over Kevin O’Leary’s Stratos project in Box Elder County. ‖ e17: Utah Office of Energy Development director Emy Lesofski told FOX 13 that the state would not allow data-center construction without responsible resource use. ‖ e18: A 9-gigawatt data-center campus was rising along Utah’s I-15 corridor as of July 2026. ‖ e19: The Department of Energy reported that Antares went critical in early June 2026, Valar’s reactor on June 18, and Deployable Energy’s Unity on June 30, making three new reactor designs critical in one month. ‖ e20: Valar Atomics CEO Isaiah Taylor said in the DOE’s criticality announcement that the site had been empty nine months earlier. ‖ e21: Valar Atomics’ stated goal of generating power by July 4, 2026, was achieved on July 1, 2026.

the demonstration generated power on July 1.

## call 79 · gnews:b680fbe2c3e9228eba33a0b4
- REF must not propose: Valar or SMR theses
### C79-a: records 11 kept of 11
  - w79-2: e10 | Tom's Hardware reported that at least 75 data center projects were delayed or cancelled in the first quarter of 2026.
  - unmatched: e1: Valar Atomics activated its Ward 250 nuclear microreactor during a live event on an unspecified date. ‖ e2: Valar Atomics announced a partnership with Nvidia to power an AI factory; the article gives no announcement date beyond publication. ‖ e3: The announced Valar Atomics–Nvidia AI factory is described as 30 MW and closed-loop, without using local water. ‖ e4: At the live event, a Valar Atomics team member plugged an Nvidia RTX desktop unit into the reactor, which was raised to 37% power to activate the Blackwell-powered PC. ‖ e5: Valar Atomics said nuclearwebsite.com runs solely on a server powered by its Ward 250 reactor. ‖ e6: Valar Atomics CEO Isiah Taylor said the reactor was producing 100 kilowatts of thermal energy, with uranium atoms fissioning at “10 to 15th power” per second, while powering an Nvidia Blackwell chip. ‖ e7: The Department of Energy said Deployable Energy's Unity and Antares Nuclear's Mark-0 had also achieved criticality; Valar Atomics claimed to be the first startup to achieve power production. ‖ e8: Amazon, Google, Microsoft, Nvidia, and Oracle had invested in nuclear technologies by 2024, according to Tom's Hardware. ‖ e9: A survey cited by Tom's Hardware found that 7 out of 10 respondents did not want a data center in their backyard. ‖ e11: Amazon, Microsoft, and Nvidia were working on technologies intended to reduce data center water use by up to 100%.

## call 210 · gnews:085b1e1a1543437edb9756d6
- REF w210-1 [core] The mechanics of NVIDIA's backstop (a take-or-pay minimum revenue guarantee, revenue share above it): the obligation question, from a research firm wi
- REF w210-2 [core] The first disclosed size of one NVIDIA backstop: $4.88bn over six years. The quantity the obligation question is settled by; Sharon's disclosure, not 
- REF w210-3 [supporting] Programme terms and the extension to data-center leases.
- REF w210-4 [supporting] The partner's expected revenue under the backstop: sizes NVIDIA's revenue share and exposure.
- REF w210-5 [supporting] A research firm's aggregate capex and debt forecasts: evidence to the capex aggregate, far out.
- REF w210-6 [supporting] A competitor runs the same structure: competition colour on the programme.
- REF w210-7 [supporting] The firm's above-consensus H2 view (no percentage here).
- REF must propose: NVIDIA's AI Compute Partnership / backstop obligation (fold_expected)
- REF must not propose: A '$7T AI debt market by 2029' thesis
- REF must not propose: Sharon AI or Firmus theses
- REF must not propose: Neocloud IRR scenarios as theses
### C210-a: records 34 kept of 34
  - w210-1: e7 | SemiAnalysis reports that Nvidia has started providing neoclouds take-or-pay minimum-revenue guarantees on GPU capacity in exchange for a share of revenue above the guarantee.
  - w210-2: e23 | SemiAnalysis says SharonAI announced a 72 MW Australian AI factory in June 2026, scaling to as many as 40,000 GB300 GPUs under a six-year backstop. ‖ e24 | SharonAI disclosed a $4.88 billion total backstop value, equivalent to an average floor of about $2.33 per GPU-hour over six years.
  - w210-3: e8 | SemiAnalysis reports that Nvidia has started backstopping data center leases. ‖ e9 | SemiAnalysis says Nvidia's backstop program typically lasts six years and commits Nvidia to purchase compute at pre-agreed prices over that period.

over backstops and others are considering the program.

  - unmatched: e1: Greenpeace East Asia and Alistair Alexander claimed that Taiwan and South Korea bear over 90% of the environmental burden from the chip industry described in the article. ‖ e2: Greenpeace East Asia and Alistair Alexander said Taiwan hosts more than 90% of global leading-edge chip manufacturing. ‖ e4: Greenpeace East Asia and Alistair Alexander said Nvidia's supply-chain emissions more than doubled over the three years before publication. ‖ e5: Greenpeace East Asia and Alistair Alexander reported a forecast for Nvidia GPU shipments to grow 26% per year; no specific target or deadline was given. ‖ e16: Greenpeace's energy team warned that South Korea may revert to coal rather than accelerate renewables; no specific quantity or date was given. ‖ e17: Ahead of Nvidia's June 24, 2026 annual meeting, shareholders proposed that the company report the lifecycle carbon emissions of its products after sale. ‖ e18: Nvidia recommended that shareholders vote against the lifecycle-emissions reporting proposal ahead of its June 24, 2026 annual meeting, calling it “too prescriptive.”

  - w83-2: e7 | The article’s authors said TSMC’s planned Fab 25 near Taichung will require at least 1 GW of power and about 100,000 tonnes of water per day. ‖ e9 | The article’s authors estimated that TSMC could consume 24% of Taiwan’s electricity by 2030, compared with over 12% in 2025.
  - unmatched: e1: The article’s authors claimed Taiwan and South Korea bear over 90% of the burden of energy, water and toxic-chemical use from new chip generations; no period was specified. ‖ e2: Jensen Huang said energy is the foundational layer of AI development; the article gives no date for the remarks. ‖ e3: The article’s authors stated that Taiwan hosts more than 90% of global leading-edge chip manufacturing; no period was specified. ‖ e5: The article’s authors said Nvidia’s supply-chain emissions more than doubled over the three years preceding publication. ‖ e6: The article reported an unspecified forecast that Nvidia GPU shipments will grow 26% per year; the forecast period and source were not identified. ‖ e8: The article’s authors said TSMC is planning 10 additional advanced fabs like Fab 25; no completion period was specified. ‖ e10: Taipower plans to build 18 gas plants with total capacity of about 18.2 GW; the article gives no completion date. ‖ e11: The article’s authors said fossil fuels account for more than 80% of Taiwan’s electricity; no period was specified. ‖ e12: The article’s authors said South Korea’s Yongin Mega-cluster plan calls for power from six LNG units totaling 3 GW by 2030, rather than renewables. ‖ e13: The article’s authors estimated that the Yongin complex will ultimately demand about 15 GW of electricity; no date was specified. ‖ e14: The article’s authors estimated that Yongin will consume about half as much water as Seoul; no period was specified. ‖ e15: Taiwan said it had secured energy supplies through the end of 2026. ‖ e16: Ahead of Nvidia’s June 24, 2026 annual meeting, shareholders proposed that the company report product lifecycle carbon emissions after sale. ‖ e17: Nvidia recommended that shareholders vote against the lifecycle-emissions reporting proposal, describing it as “too prescriptive.”
### C83-i: records 14 kept of 14
  - w83-1: e3 | Jensen Huang estimated that U.S. AI companies spend $60 of every $100 on Nvidia GPUs; no period is specified.
  - w83-2: e5 | The authors say TSMC's planned Fab 25 near Taichung will require at least 1 GW of power and about 100,000 tonnes of water per day. ‖ e7 | The authors estimate TSMC could consume 24% of Taiwan's electricity by 2030, compared with over 12% in the prior year.
  - unmatched: e1: The authors claim Taiwan and South Korea bear over 90% of the burden from advanced-chip manufacturing; no period is specified. ‖ e2: The authors say Taiwan hosts more than 90% of global leading-edge chip manufacturing; no period is specified. ‖ e4: The authors say Nvidia's supply-chain emissions more than doubled over the prior three years and forecast its GPU shipments to grow 26% per year; the forecast period is not specified. ‖ e6: The authors say TSMC operates more than two dozen fabs in Taiwan and is planning 10 additional advanced fabs like Fab 25; no completion date is specified. ‖ e8: Taipower plans 18 gas plants totaling about 18.2 GW, roughly 40% of Taiwan's current generating capacity, largely to meet semiconductor-industry demand; no completion date is stated. ‖ e9: The authors say fossil fuels supply more than 80% of Taiwan's electricity; the measurement period is not specified. ‖ e10: The authors say South Korea's Yongin Mega-cluster plan calls for six LNG units totaling 3 GW by 2030. ‖ e11: The authors estimate Yongin will ultimately require about 15 GW of electricity, roughly one-sixth of South Korea's demand, and consume about half the water used by Seoul and its 10 million residents. ‖ e12: South Korean President Lee Jae-myung said the country must move very quickly toward renewable energy; no specific target or date was given. ‖ e13: Ahead of Nvidia's June 24, 2026 annual meeting, shareholders proposed that the company report its products' post-sale lifecycle carbon emissions. ‖ e14: Nvidia recommended that shareholders vote against the lifecycle-emissions reporting proposal at its June 24, 2026 annual meeting, describing it as “too prescriptive.”

## call 15 · gnews:4d1677af37befd53ca3e7b45
- REF w15-1 [core] The mechanics of NVIDIA's obligation under the AI Compute Partnership: a backstop on unsold capacity, paid by a declining revenue share.
- REF w15-2 [core] NVIDIA's potential guarantee of the OpenAI Ohio lease: its own credit risk; first in this window.
- REF w15-3 [supporting] The filed guarantee amount: evidence to the obligation quantity.
- REF w15-4 [supporting] Partner deployment plans: demand evidence, plans not deliveries.
- REF must propose: NVIDIA's AI Compute Partnership obligation (financing_amount at FY2027 filing, or event)
- REF must propose: Whether NVIDIA signs a guarantee for OpenAI's Ohio data-center lease (yes/no, due after the item)
- REF must not propose: Sharon AI or Firmus deployments as theses
- REF must not propose: each historical equity stake or leaseback as its own thesis
### C15-a: records 17 kept of 17
  - w15-1: e1 | The Information reported on 2026-07-01 that NVIDIA was guaranteeing young cloud providers’ GPU capacity and would repurchase unsold capacity at a pre-agreed price if they failed to secure renters. ‖ e2 | The report said NVIDIA would receive a declining percentage of participating cloud providers’ revenue over each contract term.
  - w15-2: e13 | The Information previously reported that NVIDIA was negotiating to guarantee OpenAI’s lease of an Ohio data center; the report estimated the fully built-out asset could cost up to $500 billion.
  - w15-3: e17 | NVIDIA’s May 2026 regulatory filing, covering the quarter ended in April, reported that it had added $3.5 billion to guarantee customer data-center leases in exchange for rights to purchase their shares.
  - w15-4: e10 | Sharon AI planned to deploy up to 40,000 GB300 GPUs; Firmus was constructing a Batam campus expected to reach 360 MW and accommodate up to 170,000 NVIDIA GPUs.
  - w15-5: e16 | In September 2024, NVIDIA committed to purchase CoreWeave’s unsold capacity through 2032 if it failed to secure tenants; the contract was then valued at $6.3 billion.
  - unmatched: e3: The report said Firmus and Sharon AI had joined the program and that three executives with NVIDIA business ties confirmed the arrangement. ‖ e4: On 2026-07-01, NVIDIA announced a business model combining revenue sharing and credit support for AI cloud providers procuring its infrastructure. ‖ e5: The Information reported that some NVIDIA personnel internally called the initiative the “AI Compute Partnership.” ‖ e6: A NVIDIA spokesperson confirmed that the program existed. ‖ e7: NVIDIA said its model would add a share of cloud-service revenue to its standard product revenue, creating a usage-based recurring revenue stream. ‖ e8: NVIDIA positioned the framework as its “DSX AI Factory” model for continuous, cross-regional, multi-tenant accelerated-computing services. ‖ e9: Sharon AI and Firmus were identified as the first cloud providers participating in NVIDIA’s model. ‖ e11: A data-center executive characterized NVIDIA’s deals as “killing two birds with one stone.” ‖ e12: A data-center executive said a lease guarantee alone would not finance GPUs, but NVIDIA’s commitment to buy unsold facility capacity would resolve both GPU and data-center financing problems. ‖ e14: The report said NVIDIA had invested several billion dollars in equity stakes in emerging cloud providers and agreed in some cases to lease back chips, including from CoreWeave and Lambda, in transactions totaling several billion dollars. ‖ e15: The Information previously reported that NVIDIA researchers had used GPU servers leased back from Lambda.
  - cand c1 binding_obligation/financing_amount due 2027-02-28: What total amount of NVIDIA’s financing and capacity-guarantee obligations will be disclosed in the commitments note of its FY2027 annual filing?
  - cand c2 binding_obligation/event due 2027-07-01: Will NVIDIA sign a financial guarantee for OpenAI’s lease of the Ohio data center by 2027-07-01?

## call 312 · gnews:8d5c91ae833af14257a59b2b
- REF w312-1 [core] China substitution of NVIDIA accelerators: competition/share evidence for the China question (role-v18 cites this very kind of survey as a material st
- REF w312-2 [supporting] A government-led buildout with a domestic-sourcing requirement: policy and demand colour for China.
- REF w312-3 [supporting] Beijing's guidance against H20 and the expected share loss.
- REF must not propose: A thesis on the survey percentage itself
- REF must not propose: A Huawei/Hygon share thesis
### C312-a: records 9 kept of 9
  - w312-1: e1 | Chinese executives surveyed by Bloomberg Intelligence said domestic products would receive 46% of their AI-accelerator budgets over the next 12 months, up from 30% today.
  - w312-2: e6 | China is allocating roughly 2 trillion yuan (US$294 billion) to build data centres nationwide over the next five years. ‖ e7 | Bloomberg News reported that domestic companies would supply at least 80% of core technologies, including chips, for the data-centre initiative.
  - w312-3: e5 | Bloomberg Intelligence survey respondents expected Nvidia’s market share in China to shrink as H20 chips became harder to find and local firms took over; Beijing had urged Chinese technology firms not to use H20 chips.
  - w312-4: e8 | The report said a global memory-chip shortage was likely to limit growth at Chinese AI firms such as SMIC and benefit ChangXin Memory Technologies.
  - unmatched: e2: 80% of surveyed executives said their total infrastructure spending was running over budget in 2026, mostly due to the high cost of AI-related projects. ‖ e3: A large pool of respondents to the Bloomberg Intelligence survey said they were evaluating AI accelerators from Hygon Information Technology and Cambricon Technologies. ‖ e4: Bloomberg Intelligence said China’s substitution of foreign AI semiconductors with locally made chips was progressing and likely to benefit Huawei and Hygon; its survey covered 60 executives. ‖ e9: The report said bottlenecks were shifting from computing power to securing supplies of high-bandwidth memory chips.

  - w312-4: e9 | The report said a global memory-chip shortage was likely to constrain growth at Chinese AI firms such as SMIC and benefit ChangXin Memory Technologies.

  - w69-1: e7 | Nvidia estimated in its report to the U.S. government that Huawei sold 200,000–450,000 AI chips in 2024 and would sell 1 million in 2025. ‖ e8 | Nvidia estimated that by the end of 2026 SMIC would have made more than 700,000 Huawei-designed Ascend 910C chips, at a production rate of 50,000 per month.
  - w69-2: e19 | Epoch AI estimated Huawei accounted for 6% of worldwide AI chips sold in Q1 2026, or 3% after adjusting for the lower processing power of Huawei’s chips.
  - w69-3: e24 | As of July 2, 2026, the Chinese government had kept Nvidia out of the China market despite the U.S. administration clearing H200 exports.
  - unmatched: e1: In a report presented to the U.S. government last year, Nvidia said Huawei’s chip stockpile and SMIC’s growing capacity gave Huawei the flexibility to satisfy global AI chip demand. ‖ e2: Nvidia’s report to the U.S. government said Huawei production and sales would soar if Nvidia were kept out of China, without specifying a timeframe. ‖ e3: President Trump announced in December 2025 that his administration would allow Nvidia to sell H200 chips to China. ‖ e4: Gregory Allen said Huawei is a threat, but not in the way Nvidia claimed. ‖ e5: Gregory Allen said a large chip stockpile does not by itself establish marketplace competitiveness. ‖ e6: Jensen Huang said Nvidia’s China market share had fallen to zero since the Biden administration first imposed AI-chip export controls in 2022. ‖ e9: Nvidia’s report said SMIC would be producing several million Huawei 910B chips by 2027. ‖ e10: Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick estimated in congressional testimony in June 2025 that China could make 200,000 advanced chips in 2025. ‖ e11: An Nvidia spokesperson said China’s domestic ecosystem can yield and scale competitive AI systems, citing leading Chinese models optimized for domestic hardware. ‖ e12: Chris McGuire estimated in a December 2025 report that 800,000 Huawei Ascend 910C chips in 2025 would equal 5.3% of Nvidia’s total chip-production processing power that year. ‖ e13: Chris McGuire estimated that Nvidia’s performance lead would grow even if Huawei made 2 million AI chips in 2026 and 4 million in 2027. ‖ e14: Chris McGuire said Huawei did not pose a global competitive threat to Nvidia at the time of publication because Huawei’s chips were less capable and its production insufficient. ‖ e15: Chris McGuire said U.S. export controls on semiconductor manufacturing equipment had degraded China’s AI-chip manufacturing ability and largely contributed to Nvidia’s lead over Huawei. ‖ e16: Reuters reported, as cited in Nvidia’s report, that Huawei had stockpiled 2–3 million AI chips made by TSMC and obtained through illegal diversion by Sophgo. ‖ e17: Lennart Heim questioned why Huawei would obtain chips from TSMC if it could produce that many chips itself. ‖ e18: Lennart Heim said Huawei’s reported 2–3 million-chip stockpile was small relative to more than 60 million 910C-equivalent chips the U.S. would have produced from January 2025 through the end of 2026. ‖ e20: Lennart Heim said there were good arguments against export controls. ‖ e21: Gregory Allen said Nvidia had damaged its credibility on Capitol Hill and that lawmakers and the White House should be cautious about accepting its figures and interpretations at face value. ‖ e22: Huawei introduced its 950PR chip in March 2026 and was preparing to launch another chip later in 2026. ‖ e23: Lennart Heim said Huawei’s 950PR and planned chip did not match the H200’s quality because of slower memory and lagged further behind Nvidia’s Blackwell chips. ‖ e25: An Nvidia spokesperson said the company sells its first, best and most technology to the U.S. and offers competitive products in China only to commercial users vetted and approved by the U.S. government, with national security taking precedence over commercial opportunity.
